PREAMBLE
This licence allows the licensed fonts to be used, studied, modified and

licence does not require any document created using the fonts or their
derivatives to be published under this licence, as long as the primary

the fonts.

DEFINITIONS
"Font Software" refers to the set of files released by the Copyright
Holder(s) under this licence and clearly marked as such. This may
include source files, build scripts and documentation.

"Original Version" refers to the collection of Font Software components
as received under this licence.

"Modified Version" refers to any derivative made by adding to, deleting,
or substituting -- in part or in whole -- any of the components of the
Original Version, by changing formats or by porting the Font Software to
a new environment.

"Copyright Holder(s)" refers to all individuals and companies who have a
copyright ownership of the Font Software.

"Substantially Changed" refers to Modified Versions which can be easily
identified as dissimilar to the Font Software by users of the Font
Software comparing the Original Version with the Modified Version.

To "Propagate" a work means to do anything with it that, without
permission, would make you directly or secondarily liable for
infringement under applicable copyright law, except executing it on a
computer or modifying a private copy. Propagation includes copying,
distribution (with or without modification and with or without charging

rights are reserved.

(a) The Original Version must retain its name, unmodified.
(b) Modified Versions which are Substantially Changed must be renamed to
avoid use of the name of the Original Version or similar names entirely.
(c) Modified Versions which are not Substantially Changed must be
renamed to both (i) retain the name of the Original Version and (ii) add
additional naming elements to distinguish the Modified Version from the
Original Version. The name of such Modified Versions must be the name of
the Original Version, with "derivative X" where X represents the name of

Font Software shall not be used to promote, endorse or advertise any

Optional DWG bridge

`craft-dwg` uses `acadrust` 0.6.6 unmodified as a separate dependency for DWG reading and writing.
It is licensed under the Mozilla Public License 2.0. The license text and upstream third-party
notices are retained in `licenses/dwg/`; the crate is available from crates.io and
https://github.com/hakanaktt/acadrust.

The byte-level bridge source was moved from CADCraft `crates/dwg` at revision
`664837df8925f932e90a7ae37c58d2f54e7a1320`. The bridge itself is MIT OR Apache-2.0; its only
MPL-2.0 component is the unmodified `acadrust` dependency.

list (author, source, license) is in ATTRIBUTION.md.

  contributors, MIT OR Apache-2.0; the port was modified by the LightCraft contributors (2026).

Optional PDF writer

Krilla 0.8.2 (https://github.com/LaurenzV/krilla), MIT OR Apache-2.0.
Upstream licence texts are retained in licenses/pdf-writer.
The optional writer embeds Compact ICC Profiles by Clinton Ingram
(https://github.com/saucecontrol/Compact-ICC-Profiles), under CC0-1.0.
See licenses/pdf-writer/LICENSE-ICC.txt and ATTRIBUTION.md for sources and hashes.
The writer adds no font files. Its four existing ICC profiles are retained under vendor/krilla/icc.

Krilla is vendored with the local patches in vendor/krilla/patches; see vendor/krilla/README.craft.md
and vendor/krilla/SOURCE.json. Its upstream MIT/Apache-2.0 licences and CC0 profiles are unchanged.

Optional PDF reader

Hayro 0.7.1, hayro-interpret 0.7.0, hayro-syntax 0.7.2, hayro-jbig2 0.3.0, hayro-cmap 0.1.0
and hayro-postscript 0.1.0 (https://github.com/LaurenzV/hayro), MIT OR Apache-2.0, retain their
upstream licence files. The first five are patched sources transferred from PdfCraft
(https://github.com/storytold/pdfcraft): hayro-cmap from revision
a2ec27b4775f53c40cad3456936d9406611e3a8d, the others from revision
ec0a965074f376a5bf610435948e4e5184bf3299. hayro-postscript is the crates.io 0.1.0 package
(upstream revision a2354aebaf86f5ab13e5fd6b6b0521306488bf9d) with one local patch.
See vendor/README.craft-reader.md and each crate's SOURCE.json for provenance and local changes.
The hayro-interpret plate mode (separations, overprint and output-intent simulation) is ported
from PdfCraft pull request #2770 by Guillem Hermida, commit
6fcf2a4aca29e943e28cb69017ff8f87d00656c6 (https://github.com/storytold/pdfcraft/pull/2770),
MIT OR Apache-2.0; crates/pdf/tests/plates.rs and tests/support/synthetic_icc.rs adapt its tests.
Knockout transparency groups in hayro-interpret and hayro are ported from PdfCraft pull request
#2974 by mtlcl410, commit bae69819594a6385a027fec1f67e6b3c36c259b4
(https://github.com/storytold/pdfcraft/pull/2974), MIT OR Apache-2.0;
crates/pdf/tests/knockout.rs adapts its test.
The reader retains the existing Foxit/PDFium standard-14 font assets (BSD-3-Clause),
Compact ICC Profiles by Clinton Ingram (CC0-1.0), and dependency CMaps (BSD-3-Clause).
Their licences and per-file hashes are retained in vendor/hayro-interpret/assets,
vendor/hayro-cmap/assets, licenses/pdf-backend and ATTRIBUTION.md. These are existing reader dependency assets;
applications retain their own document-font selection and licensing policy.

linesweeper

linesweeper 0.5.0 (https://crates.io/crates/linesweeper/0.5.0), MIT OR Apache-2.0, is stored in
vendor/linesweeper with its upstream license files and local patches, to src/sweep/sweep_line.rs,
src/curve/mod.rs, src/curve/line.rs and docs/sweep.typ. Its manifest names Joe Neeman as author, and
its LICENSE-MIT reads "Copyright (c) 2018 Raph Levien". See vendor/linesweeper/README.craft.md and
vendor/linesweeper/SOURCE.json for provenance, the local patches and the files left out.

Platform lifecycle source provenance

The optional crates/platform services adapt the preserved local platform candidate
9b135779a1e7136438a217c18afe1693ea23f1ab (dialog outcomes and browser resource ownership),
and current application implementations:
- PdfCraft crates/platform/src/desktop_theme.rs, a8cd797a917b1007544575c7280c9a0ce6a17e8f.
- CADCraft apps/cadcraft/src/desktop_theme.rs, 0cc66ad3066e71f2c3d4cacb7de905e79ba1c95b.
- ArtCraft Launcher apps/artcraft-launcher/src/system_theme.rs, f1676dfb41c226b5be490721f486ee6427419d80.
- Earlier PhotoCraft, FilmCraft and EffectCraft appearance services at revisions
  0c72d95425dece90ef9a1cceb49e3315c96e22d5, 794a25fc9ed2e3f6fab51a254625bc9b99a88015
  and 1b4d47f34f2b1c1b4a89f4b7348889def8fd648b respectively.
Sources: https://github.com/storytold/pdfcraft, https://github.com/storytold/cadcraft,
https://github.com/storytold/craft-launcher, https://github.com/storytold/photocraft,
https://github.com/storytold/filmcraft, https://github.com/storytold/effectcraft.
Copyright (c) 2026 ArtCraft Team and the respective application and craft-libs contributors.
All adapted source is licensed MIT OR Apache-2.0.

The Canon CRW decoder in crates/crw (src/lib.rs, src/tables.rs, src/tests.rs, tests/) and its
derivation record (crates/crw/clean-room/) come from:
- LightCraft, crates/raw/src/vendor/crw.rs, crates/raw/src/vendor/crw_tables.rs,
  crates/raw/tests/crw_oracle.rs, crates/raw/tests/it/vendor_robust.rs and clean-room/crw/,
  https://github.com/storytold/lightcraft/pull/3021, revision 6ca439b5155820d2befeacd81ec0c6a80b2bd3bc
  (decoder ported at 70c755e42a65661401142819278d51b0c5977f7f; the record is identical at both).
  https://github.com/storytold/lightcraft
  Copyright (c) 2026 ArtCraft Team and the LightCraft contributors.
Its white-level plateau estimate (saturation_plateau) is LightCraft's vendor::white_from_data at the
same revision. The CRW compression was derived clean-room from CC0 sample files and the
maintainer's own photographs by black-box comparison with a reference decoder's command-line
output; no raw-decoder source was consulted (crates/crw/clean-room/sources.md).

crates/audio/src/alac/ (the ALAC decoder and encoder) is ported from Apple's ALAC reference
implementation, https://github.com/macosforge/alac at c38887c5c5e64a4b31108733bd79ca9b2496d987
(codec/), Copyright (c) 2011 Apple Inc., licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0
(LICENSE-APACHE). The files are modified: translated to Rust, with bounds checks on hostile input,
wrapping integer arithmetic, the unrolled predictor and per-width matrix routines folded into one,
and the average bit rate filled in. Those files are Apache-2.0 only.

Original src/fdct.rs opening notice

/*
 * Ported from mozjpeg to rust
 *
 * This file was part of the Independent JPEG Group's software:
 * Copyright (C) 1991-1996, Thomas G. Lane.
 * libjpeg-turbo Modifications:
 * Copyright (C) 2015, 2020, D. R. Commander.
 *
 * Conditions of distribution and use:
 * In plain English:
 *
 * 1. We don't promise that this software works.  (But if you find any bugs,
 *    please let us know!)
 * 2. You can use this software for whatever you want.  You don't have to pay us.
 * 3. You may not pretend that you wrote this software.  If you use it in a
 *    program, you must acknowledge somewhere in your documentation that
 *    you've used the IJG code.
 *
 * In legalese:
 *
 * The authors make NO WARRANTY or representation, either express or implied,
 * with respect to this software, its quality, accuracy, merchantability, or
 * fitness for a particular purpose.  This software is provided "AS IS", and you,
 * its user, assume the entire risk as to its quality and accuracy.
 *
 * This software is copyright (C) 1991-2020, Thomas G. Lane, Guido Vollbeding.
 * All Rights Reserved except as specified below.
 *
 * Permission is hereby granted to use, copy, modify, and distribute this
 * software (or portions thereof) for any purpose, without fee, subject to these
 * conditions:
 * (1) If any part of the source code for this software is distributed, then this
 * README file must be included, with this copyright and no-warranty notice
 * unaltered; and any additions, deletions, or changes to the original files
 * must be clearly indicated in accompanying documentation.
 * (2) If only executable code is distributed, then the accompanying
 * documentation must state that "this software is based in part on the work of
 * the Independent JPEG Group".
 * (3) Permission for use of this software is granted only if the user accepts
 * full responsibility for any undesirable consequences; the authors accept
 * NO LIABILITY for damages of any kind.
 *
 * These conditions apply to any software derived from or based on the IJG code,
 * not just to the unmodified library.  If you use our work, you ought to
 * acknowledge us.
 *
 * Permission is NOT granted for the use of any IJG author's name or company name
 * in advertising or publicity relating to this software or products derived from
 * it.  This software may be referred to only as "the Independent JPEG Group's
 * software".
 *
 * We specifically permit and encourage the use of this software as the basis of
 * commercial products, provided that all warranty or liability claims are
 * assumed by the product vendor.
 *
 * This file contains a slower but more accurate integer implementation of the
 * forward DCT (Discrete Cosine Transform).
 *
 * A 2-D DCT can be done by 1-D DCT on each row followed by 1-D DCT
 * on each column.  Direct algorithms are also available, but they are
 * much more complex and seem not to be any faster when reduced to code.
 *
 * This implementation is based on an algorithm described in
 *   C. Loeffler, A. Ligtenberg and G. Moschytz, "Practical Fast 1-D DCT
 *   Algorithms with 11 Multiplications", Proc. Int'l. Conf. on Acoustics,
 *   Speech, and Signal Processing 1989 (ICASSP '89), pp. 988-991.
 * The primary algorithm described there uses 11 multiplies and 29 adds.
 * We use their alternate method with 12 multiplies and 32 adds.
 * The advantage of this method is that no data path contains more than one
 * multiplication; this allows a very simple and accurate implementation in
 * scaled fixed-point arithmetic, with a minimal number of shifts.
 */

Original src/avx2/fdct.rs opening notice

/*
 * Ported from mozjpeg / jfdctint-avx2.asm to rust
 * Copyright 2009 Pierre Ossman <ossman@cendio.se> for Cendio AB
 * Copyright (C) 2009, 2016, 2018, 2020, D. R. Commander.
 *
 * Based on the x86 SIMD extension for IJG JPEG library
 * Copyright (C) 1999-2006, MIYASAKA Masaru.
 */

The .cdr reader (crates/cdr, moved from VectorCraft crates/cdr) reads the record layouts that the
Kaitai Struct specification coreldraw_cdr.ksy (https://github.com/kaitai-io/coreldraw_cdr.ksy,
MIT License) and its fork by Zach Wolfe (https://github.com/filestar/coreldraw_cdr.ksy, branch
new_stuff, MIT License) describe. No part of either specification is copied into the crate; the
license text is in crates/cdr/LICENSE-kaitai.txt.

The Y4M range-backed source in crates/media-source/src/y4m.rs adapts FilmCraft's
original implementation (3f137a7ed420cac79b32145f5243b7f98b31f71c), licensed
MIT OR Apache-2.0. Source notice retained:
Copyright (c) 2026 ArtCraft Team and the FilmCraft contributors.

## Binary Apple property lists

`crates/plist` is extracted from `storytold/craft-anything` (`crates/formats/src/plist.rs` and checked byte reads), at source main `88d1d9cac852aec1c7fd97886db451e005e49d2a`. MIT OR Apache-2.0; source license texts are retained in the crate. Primary import Git authors preserve Brandon Thomas, BFlat, RRFZ and Afloat. Regression imports preserve BFlat. XML, JSON projection, calendar formatting and app policy were not imported into the public API.

`crates/tga/src/metadata_tests.rs` and metadata-boundary helpers in `crates/tga/src/lib.rs` are adapted from nuclearspike's FilmCraft #1932 at `e7842f76f887005c460c64a8ab51d59d086e6f27` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Synthetic fixtures are original contributor test data; primary Git author retained.
The XML reader in `crates/plist/src/xml.rs` and its base64 compatibility helper are ported from Craft Anything `8f3b775157d331dd31cfa4da911e26d8856b8c77` (same MIT OR Apache-2.0 source licenses). Brandon Thomas is the primary import author; retained hardening credits include Afloat, BFlat, Rahul, RRFZ and NetterSilver. The finite-real fix preserves gmrmad1 as author from Craft Anything #1905 (`d85e104f`); quoted-attribute/line-ending regressions preserve RRFZ. Bounds/adaptation and bounded entity scanning are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes. App-specific JSON projections and keyed-archive interpretation remain outside this crate.

## Score serializers

`crates/score` extracts SoundCraft MusicXML and SVG score writing from `crates/engine/src/score.rs` at main `648ed9db9a0e98cc55643babffac381521a556c8`, with per-pitch ties adapted from [SoundCraft #182](https://github.com/storytold/soundcraft/pull/182) at `21cb618d93ec0e8d4771ac534994de8d5996ff10`. Source is MIT OR Apache-2.0. Import commits preserve Brandon Thomas, NetterSilver, Samuel Bacaro and Vikram Bhaskaran as primary authors of their respective changes. The consumer retains session mapping and layout. Shared API wiring, validation and focused tests are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes.

## Source-timed MIDI compatibility

`crates/midi/src/source_timed.rs` is extracted from LoopCraft `crates/midi/src/lib.rs` at `884e67c24b9b68badf6a4a4f7d62492f3673d354` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). The recorded source history for the extracted file is Dylan M. Taylor (`0d150c1f`, `440bc7ed`, `9e50523d`); Git authorship is preserved. It includes the original hostile-file, SMPTE, timing and round-trip tests. UTF-8 truncation and event-budget suffix repairs are subsequent changes. The module is an application-independent source-timed model; it does not depend on LoopCraft.
## Non-seeking WAV primitives

`crates/audio/src/wav_stream.rs` is extracted from AniCraft `crates/audio/src/wav.rs` at `d875a7feb250e6ab06aa28a37fb78a0699e88330` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Tanish Manku is retained as the primary import author, from original commit `30c121d269a49d9abe54a3834ed4aa0201542535`. Odd-data padding accounting, shared API documentation and regression tests are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes.

## Audio DSP

`crates/audio-dsp` combines FilmCraft `crates/audio-dsp` at `3f137a7ed420cac79b32145f5243b7f98b31f71c` and SoundCraft `crates/dsp` at `648ed9db9a0e98cc55643babffac381521a556c8` (both MIT OR Apache-2.0). `crates/audio-dsp/NOTICE` keeps the copyright notices of both projects.

## GIMP palette reader

`crates/palette` extracts the GPL text parser from AniCraft `crates/doc/src/swatch.rs` at `d875a7feb250e6ab06aa28a37fb78a0699e88330` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), introduced by Tanish Manku in `a64130a9da9bdde545463b852d95d6a6774b1dfd` (AniCraft #270). The import preserves Tanish as primary author. Neutral RGB/name adaptation separates application display-name sanitization and the `Untitled` policy; validation tests and integration are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes.

The legacy ZIP Zstandard method-20 mapping and bounded-read regression in `crates/zip/src/lenient` are ported from RRFZ's Craft Anything commit `29badae7c9280fdf4c24795927bf3a91f51694c6` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). RRFZ remains the primary Git author. Firmware scanner detection remains app-owned and is not part of this port.
## TAR/CPIO compatibility updates

The old GNU sparse-extension traversal, GNU CRC-symlink compatibility and PAX-size precedence fixes and their regression tests in `crates/tar` are ported from RRFZ's Craft Anything PRs #1973 (`8ea2ec66a1bc80f0420f3695baac877a0cb9b9a5`), #1984 (`b226a475f7dce9710e3af7a069386fe7a9720df7`) and #2007 (`d29465c20b0d570ea4973be512a0cb4e212bb60f`). Source is MIT OR Apache-2.0; RRFZ remains the primary Git author of each port. The PAX update adapts to the shared parser's existing `PaxOverrides` structure without changing the source policy.

## Aligned stored ZIP entries

The USDZ alignment requirement and compatibility fixture for `Writer::add_stored_aligned` come from TextureCraft `crates/formats/src/mesh_export/usd.rs`, introduced by bflatastic in `bdbb52c9` and maintained through `9eb20086` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). The shared API and alignment validation are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor work. Scene/material mapping remains in TextureCraft.

The raw gradient wire ordering in `craft-lottie::Gradient::flat_values` follows
AniCraft's Lottie exporter by Tanish Manku (`63b39355`, `b9258b7b`), MIT OR Apache-2.0.
Sanitization, animation fitting and numeric output policy remain caller-owned.

## Screen Video key frames

`crates/video/src/screen_video.rs` extracts AniCraft `crates/xfl/src/video.rs` codec-3 decoding and its synthetic partial-block fixture, introduced by Tanish Manku in `1f3e603b7ff53a3da364e41ec4da8d43dc83aab0` with pixel-loop cleanup `2b7571363b4b647266df758f95b6e397021cfd4d` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Import commits preserve Tanish as primary author. Caller-owned bounds and neutral RGBA output separate codec work from the consuming app's FLV first-frame/report policy. Boundary tests and integration are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes.

## Source Map v3 parser and lookup

`crates/sourcemap` extracts the bounded parser, source inventory and generated-position lookup from Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/sourcemap.rs` at `e5c9c94106747ed44b9a3fb0decce867c2285f93` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Core-history ports preserve Brandon Thomas, BFlat, Afloat, RRFZ, Nicolas Bohn and Alex Joaquim Pereira as primary authors, with exact source revisions in each commit. Six regression files preserve their source authors; nine core unit tests retain the original tests. App annotation scanning, data URLs, base64 and file I/O are intentionally not imported. Workspace wiring is a subsequent change; existing limits and errors are unchanged.
## JSONC span-preserving editing

`crates/jsonc` is extracted from Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/jsonc.rs` at `e5c9c94106747ed44b9a3fb0decce867c2285f93` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). The original implementation and hardening by BFlat, newline preservation by NetterSilver, and subsequent RRFZ/Afloat correctness fixes retain separate primary-author port commits with immutable source revisions. Shared API wiring and optional input-byte admission are subsequent changes. Compatibility parsing and all existing formatting/editing tests are retained; application file I/O and policy are not imported.

## XFA XML DOM

`crates/xfa/src/xml.rs` is extracted from PDFCraft at `271e479c53723b1b294c866d5cb2f27604601b29`, originally by Dylan M. Taylor (`eead4541`, `7d44e654`), MIT OR Apache-2.0. The initial extraction changes visibility and module boundaries; subsequent namespace-retention budget hardening intentionally tightens resource admission. PDF/layout/form policy remains in the app.

## Java class inspection

`crates/java-class` extracts Craft Anything `java_class.rs` and `java_ops.rs` at `5979f9b13f16dcf1df3c7ae7f8e7735f452866f2` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), preserving separate primary-author source-history ports by Brandon Thomas, BFlat, Afloat and RRFZ. The checked byte-reader, modified UTF-8 unit decoder, descriptor display helper and synthetic class fixtures come from the same source snapshot. Seven regression files retain their source authors. Neutral error wiring and shared crate integration are subsequent changes. No JAR archive, application I/O or bytecode execution is imported.

## WebAssembly inspection

`crates/wasm` extracts Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/wasm.rs` at `5979f9b13f16dcf1df3c7ae7f8e7735f452866f2`, with its checked byte-reader subset and original synthetic WASM/testkit fixtures (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Eight core-history ports preserve Brandon Thomas, Afloat and RRFZ as primary authors; component, repeated-custom-section and referenced-string regression imports retain source authors and commit references. Fixtures come from `crates/testkit/src/formats.rs` and deterministic mutation helpers from `crates/testkit/src/lib.rs`; no third-party binaries. Shared error naming and workspace wiring are subsequent changes. Existing inspection limits and report behavior are unchanged; this crate validates/inspects/renders, never executes modules.

## Packet capture and protocol inspection

`crates/pcap` extracts Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/pcap.rs`, its checked byte reader primitives and synthetic packet fixtures/regressions at `5979f9b13f16dcf1df3c7ae7f8e7735f452866f2` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Brandon Thomas introduced the implementation, byte reader and fixtures in `f64ae3bf3c8863a982af1304e06de20653803968`. Subsequent fixes by phatloi0010, GGWA, BFlat, Afloat, NetterSilver, Max Mittelstädt and RRFZ retain separate primary-author source-history commits with immutable source revisions. Shared error/API wiring and chrono-based timestamp formatting with exhaustive day-boundary parity tests are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes. No packet reassembly, decryption, live capture or app analysis policy is imported.
## HTTP Archive reader

`crates/har` preserves Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/har.rs` from `5979f9b13f16dcf1df3c7ae7f8e7735f452866f2` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Brandon Thomas introduced the typed reader in `f64ae3bf3c8863a982af1304e06de20653803968`; BFlat added UTF-8 BOM compatibility in `cb0bb9d8fa4536ba44ef2c86f5ed759b879cc2d3`. Both source snapshots retain primary author attribution. Shared wiring, explicit byte admission and its tests are subsequent Dylan M. Taylor changes. Redaction and analysis remain app-owned.

## Dalvik DEX inspection

`crates/dex` extracts Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/dex.rs` and `dex_ops.rs` at `5979f9b13f16dcf1df3c7ae7f8e7735f452866f2` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Five source-history ports preserve Brandon Thomas, BFlat and Afloat as primary authors. Five regression imports retain BFlat/Afloat authors and exact source revisions. The checked byte-reader/MUTF-8 subset and synthetic DEX fixture come from that app's `bytes.rs` and `testkit/src/android.rs`; deterministic noise/mutations come from its testkit. No application file I/O, APK logic or execution is imported. Shared neutral errors and workspace wiring are subsequent changes; existing per-file strings, member/handler and bytecode payload budgets are retained.

## EVM bytecode inspection

`crates/evm` extracts Craft Anything `crates/formats/src/evm.rs` at `a31e995858f4312e0f1d59a007112da191ef2d26` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). Eight separate source-history ports preserve Brandon Thomas, Afloat, RRFZ and NetterSilver as primary authors with immutable source references. Synthetic EVM fixtures and deterministic mutation helpers come from the same app source; regression files retain their source provenance. Neutral errors and workspace integration are subsequent changes. No code execution or transaction behavior is imported.

## XFDF field reader

`crates/xfdf` extracts PDFCraft field reading from #5937 refresh `cb564fd827f572818e9ddb0ea5989a432b1f39fd` (MIT OR Apache-2.0). App-side annotation and form policy remains local; source history is retained in the original repository.

Retained XFDF source contributors (move-aware blame): Brandon Thomas, Marco Arnulfo, pkibiego and storybored379; module split by aiongg. Subsequent budget/integration work by Dylan M. Taylor.

## Y4M range-backed playback

`crates/media-source/src/y4m.rs` adapts the in-house [FilmCraft Y4mSource at 3f137a7e](https://github.com/storytold/filmcraft/blob/3f137a7ed420cac79b32145f5243b7f98b31f71c/crates/codecs/src/y4m.rs), MIT OR Apache-2.0. Original playback by Lloyd Nicholson ([FilmCraft #1303](https://github.com/storytold/filmcraft/pull/1303)); shared-parser integration by Dylan M. Taylor ([FilmCraft #4862](https://github.com/storytold/filmcraft/pull/4862)). The adaptation changes source/frame types and declines payloads above the shared media allocation ceiling before playback. The parser and its previous header-safety work remain in `craft-y4m` ([craft-libs #933](https://github.com/storytold/craft-libs/pull/933)).

`crates/media-source/tests/it/y4m.rs` contains original repository-authored synthetic test data under MIT OR Apache-2.0: literal YUV samples and a sparse reader that reports only the length of an oversized frame. It does not use external media or decoder output.
